ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-26-sejm-50-2.u29	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! I answer the questions asked. Firstly, as regards Mrs Rosa's first question, it is true that over the years up to 2029 the operating costs of the power market will be PLN 29 billion, except that they will not be borne by any additional resources, but only from the current energy price will be a separate charge. The point is that this power charge, which is already at the price of the energy market, operates and dies at general costs, is used for different purposes. We want to lead to a choice and through legislation to introduce that this is a fee to support investment, to cover this gap in costs that occur, in a situation where a conventional power plant is waiting for a moment when there will be no wind when there will be less sunshine. We have less wind than other countries in Europe, less sunshine and, as far as renewable energy is concerned, we are in the same situation. [[Learn a little bit.]] Unfortunately, therefore, we need to respond to this so that our industry can function so that Poland can grow, and it is to a greater extent that renewable energy sources are subsidised. [[Coal.]] We do, we auction for renewable energy sources. [[Bravo.]] We have decided to implement the findings of the European Commission, the European Union, to which Poland has joined within the European Union. We're making these arrangements. But in order to do so, we need energy security, the ability to work, the functioning of family farms and the functioning of the economy, so that there is a continuous supply of electricity. If we do not put in place the power market, we will not secure it, the situation is that if we make investments in this situation, these investments from the point of view of being able to return on conventional energy sources are expensive, because they are as if they are not fully financially safe, because the power plant, while waiting, must increase the cost of employees' wages, must bear the cost of 40% She must be burning up, she must be walking, and therefore these costs must be covered. We are drawing this amount from the current electricity price and it will only be used to support these investments. So the total support, even present, of renewable energy is much, much greater than the support under the current fee. That's one thing. But the second thing is that if we put in a power market and we bring about stability, that means that if we're guaranteed to cover lost costs, energy will be able to get much cheaper credits, because they'll be 100 percent sure when it comes to return. So there will be savings. It's about saving money because we're not gonna have a blackout threat because it's gonna be a brave power plant. All of this together counted by 2035 gives us PLN 55 billion in savings. So let's see, here's 29 billion and here's 55 billion. Even from a point of view... [[51.]] Savings are 55 billion and costs 30 billion. The next issue is that the energy market, as a certain economic function, will also result in investment growth and economic growth. So from this point of view, this is also an extremely important element of our economic development. The third issue is the fees that are raised as part of the price of energy, but here they are shown. These charges in the case of the group approx. 3 million of the least wealthy farms will be below PLN 2 per month, and in the case of the richest farms, about PLN 9 per month. [[Look at the bills, man.]] So even from that point of view, even though it's not a new charge, it's an old one, it's not a threat, it's not a threat, and it's a guarantee of energy security, power certainty and that we're going to be covered in electricity as well... Secretary of State at the Ministry of Family, Labour and Social Policy Stanisław Szwed: And now I am going specifically to the solutions that concern the draft revision of Directive 96/71. To start with: only our ministry recently, I have a detailed list - 45 meetings, including with ministers, with prime ministers, with all who have an impact on the decision on this directive. First thing: At the meeting of the Employment and Social Policy Council (EPSCO) on 23 October, Member States adopted the position of the Council, so-called. the general approach with regard to the draft amendment to Directive 96/71 of the European Community on the posting of workers in the framework of the provision of services, the opposition of Poland, Hungary, Latvia and Lithuania, and the abstention from the vote of Croatia, Ireland and the United Kingdom, all of which count equally. Poland did not support the agreement reached in the Council due to unsatisfactory road transport solutions, which were one of the key elements of the negotiations. Transport has not been excluded from the scope of the Directive, while the Council's adopted position provides for the suspension of the application to road transport of the amended provisions on posting until the entry into force of the sectoral regulations proposed by the European Commission in the framework of the mobility package. It is also important that the EPSCO Council has decided to move transport rules to the mobility package negotiations to be conducted in the Transport Council. It is worth emphasizing that, given the doubts of many Member States, Commissioner Thyssen has made it clear that transit is not covered by the Posting of Workers Directive. In the opinion of Poland, the inclusion of transport by rules of posting of workers will have a negative impact not only on the functioning of the transport industry, but also on the free movement of goods and consequently on the competitiveness of the European economy. One of the conditions for compromise is that it has been moved to transport negotiations in the Transport Council. As I say, the ball is still in the game, this case is still to be played, also in the sense of referring to other records that occur in this respect. The next issue concerns periods. In addition to the transport issue, Poland's negative position was also affected by the proposal concerning the so-called. long-term posting. Poland was in favour of a 24-month posting period. During the negotiations, it was agreed that, as a rule, the posting would last 12 months, but by means of a reasoned notification by the service provider it would be extended to 18 months. This solution is a compromise between 12 and 24. In addition, 6 months will be from the vending machine if there is an employer's request. Then it'll be 18 months from the vending machine. The other elements of the adopted Council position mostly meet the expectations of our country. This is, among others, the case. the question of compromise in terms of extending the transposition period of the Directive. Transposition of the Directive - 3 years, implementation - 4 years, i.e. we have a 4 year transitional period for the implementation of the Directive. The very important thing that we have managed to maintain is the removal of the rules on subcontracting and the so-called. the envisaged period of posting. We also put these demands as a very important element. Another very important issue that has been adopted according to our expectations: the costs of accommodation, food and transport are covered in accordance with the laws of the sending State and not as other host countries have claimed. This is an important decision. [[For 12 months.]] 12 plus 6, which is 18 months you can. But, of course, this issue is still to be negotiated. The next issue is compliance with the rules and the issue of easing the employer's liability rules where he has complied with incomplete or incorrect information from the employment bank published on the official national website. In this case, the provision will work in favour of the employer. An important thing to say right now. The adoption of the general approach is not the end of the negotiations, but opens the next stage of work and will be the basis for negotiations with the European Parliament, whose position is very much less favourable than that negotiated by the government. This is yet to be achieved by the European Commission, which is three bodies: the European Parliament, the European Commission and the Council in the context of the negotiations. The Presidency in the negotiations today covers Estonia, after this period, after the new year it will be Bulgaria. ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-26-sejm-50-2.u64	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! On 8 June, the Sejm, as you have already pointed out, adopted a law on how to determine the minimum basic salary of medical professionals employed in medicinal entities. The main objective of this regulation was to secure minimum salaries mainly for medical workers, because we are aware that there are major problems with securing the functioning of medicinal entities, staffing equipment, professional medical staff. It is clear that some qualified medical professionals did not work in Poland, using the possibility of doing this profession in other countries of the European Union, where the salary was much better than in Poland. In this respect, among others, encourage medical workers to stay in Poland, one of such encouraging elements was the draft law. We were aware that there were large disparities in the remuneration of medical workers, even within the same professional groups, which involved a great sense of injustice for individual workers. That is why the government has undertaken this effort to regulate minimum wages. I would like to stress that this law does not constitute a wage net or a qualifier. It is a law that regulates the minimum basic remuneration. This is different from the Act on the Minimum Compensation of Workers, that not only do we regulate the minimum basic remuneration, but employees also have additional benefits associated with the consequences of fixing this minimum remuneration. This was the aim, this law was primarily dedicated to medical workers defined in the Act on Medical Activity, i.e. to those who legitimise themselves with professional qualifications for the provision of health services, who participate directly in the provision of health services. This regulation sets out in detail the level of minimum remuneration in the different groups of professional medical professionals and the way to reach this minimum remuneration. Regulation of the Art. 3 is of a nature and may also be the basis for employees' claims if the levels of remuneration resulting from that law are not maintained. On the other hand, the issue of other workers, non-medical workers, has been regulated in Article. 5 of the Act, where the legislator has imposed an obligation on the head of the medicinal entity to include the rest of the staff in those regulations, the provision of which is Article. 5 does not have a constituent character. Depending on the medical operator's ability and how these agreements are negotiated with the trade union organisations, other workers may also be subject to specific wage regulation. Why didn't we undertake the effort to regulate all our employees? Since in the case of other workers, non-medical workers who are employed in medicinal entities and pursue their profession, these professions are not entirely attributable to the so-called. a relevant feature that is characteristic of a particular profession. It is difficult for us to determine what is different about work, for example. a doorman or, let's say, a caretaker working in a medical facility from the employee's work, e.g. At the office or at the post office. So here it is difficult to find a feature that distinguishes this worker and would give the opportunity to increase his salary only because he works in a medicinal entity. Nevertheless, I would like to stress that there is such a statutory delegation for directors of medicinal entities. On the basis of the article. 5 non-medical workers may also be covered by this regulation. I would also like to stress that there has been a clear increase in health expenditure, the resources allocated to the financing of health benefits under the NFZ. We were assessing the effects of this regulation. In 2017, out of these increased expenditures on health care, the measures that are transferred to medicinal products, approx. 16.0% is consumed to increase the salaries of employees. There is therefore a chance to propose an increase in salaries from these increased incomes also to other professional groups. I would also like to stress that there are also work, discussions, discussions within the Council of Social Dialogue on this law, because from the social side there are various kinds of proposals and opinions aimed in some sense at modeling this law. This discussion is ongoing. There have already been further meetings on this bill. There may be some modifications to this bill. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-26-sejm-50-2.u80	Thank you. Dear Marshal! Minister! Media reports and consumers have shown for a long time that food produced by the same producers differs in quality, composition, consistency, smell, taste in terms of its subsequent sales on store shelves in Western Europe and Poland. Food often looks the same when it comes to packaging, but after consummation or, often, reading the composition of the product, it turns out that it differs unfortunately to the disadvantage of buyers in Poland. In view of this dual quality of consumer products, which we are undoubtedly dealing with, I would like to ask the Minister whether efforts are being made to ensure that the products that reach our shops are as good quality as those sold in western Europe. Thank you.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-26-sejm-50-2.u81	Thank you very much. I ask you to answer the question of the Secretary of State at the Ministry of Agriculture and Rural Development of Mr Jack Bogucki. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-26-sejm-50-2.u82	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! To answer the question of Members, I would like to inform you that the problem of double food quality has been raised by members of the Visegrad Group, by representatives of countries neighbouring Poland: the Czech Republic, Slovakia. Hungary and Slovenia also began to raise this problem, but they have also participated in this activity since the beginning of the Polish agricultural resort. Several meetings of the Visegrad Group have already been held at the level of agricultural ministers. Recently, a meeting was held called the Consumer Summit in Bratislava, where Prime Minister Beata Szydło took part. During this meeting, the Prime Minister, on behalf of the whole government, not only of the Minister for Agriculture himself, but of the whole government, strongly condemned the offer of products in the common market with varying quality characteristics depending on the country in which these products are offered. The problem is quite complicated because producers, especially producers offering products in many European and global markets, so it is corporations, large international companies, who are trying to explain this diversity by different consumer tastes, different consumer expectations. But the research that has been carried out so far in our neighbours' countries, of course very random research, because comparative material does not exist in this area, shows that at least some of these differences are not due to consumer habits, consumer expectations, but simply from an attempt to propose a brand that is recognizable, a brand that operates in the common market for consumers from Eastern Europe of a poorer quality of products. not always at a lower price. In Poland this problem is a little less visible in the case of food, because the vast majority of basic food hitting the Polish market comes from Polish, native companies not operating on world markets under the same brand. So, in the case of basic agri-food products, we do not see this problem, but of course this problem exists also in the case of the supply of global brands, pan-European brands to the Polish market. On request, e.g. Poland and other countries of the Visegrad Group, Eastern Europe, more broadly, because Slovenia is not a member of the Visegrad Group, the European Commission has addressed this problem. And the first such finding, which gives the opportunity to deal with this problem, concerns the development of a research system that would allow comparisons of their performance in individual countries and the exchange of information between inspection services, national inspections. Without such joint action at European level, we cannot deal with this problem because, necessarily, if we are talking about double quality, what goes on the Polish market does not reflect, in those other products present on the Polish market, products that need to be compared are on the markets of other countries. So in order for research to be comprehensive and wide-ranging, and to address the whole problem, it must be carried out in every country of the European Union and it must be universal. So that we are aware of this problem. Although this type of research has not been carried out in Poland so far, there have also been practically no complaints from consumers concerning agri-food products of double quality, but this does not mean that we as resort and our control services do not see or see this problem. This problem has been discussed several times in Poland, including in the public sector, but this concerns not agri-food products, but other market products, because we can deal with double quality not only in the agri-food sector, although in the agri-food sector this problem carries the greatest danger and has the greatest negative effects. However, it must be remembered that any product that enters the market of any European country, including Poland, must meet the quality requirements of EU legislation. So there is no situation here that there are products here that do not meet the requirements of European law and Polish law. We are talking about such a problem that under the brand recognizable, well-known, advertised in many countries other quality products come to the market of Western Europe, and other quality can reach the market of Eastern Europe, including the Polish market. And with this problem, the Ministry of Agriculture, but also the government as a whole [[♪ The bell ♪]] It's measured. We are pushing very hard on the European Commission to address this problem. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-26-sejm-50-2.u110	Thank you very much. Please respond to the Minister.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-26-sejm-50-2.u111	Dear Marshal, High Seym! In response to the questions asked about the possibilities offered by the National Property Resources Act in the context of the achievement of the most important objective, namely to build housing for people who today cannot afford to buy their own apartment due to lack of savings of adequate amount or credit, I would like to present the following information. The National Property Resources Act entered into force on 11 September of this year. It gives a number of legal instruments and tools that allow for effective, first, real estate, so to speak, to be available, secondly, to be used for housing purposes in different formats. When it comes to the question of the first question, which is how the organization looks today, it is a good thing that we have one and a half months since the entry into force of this law. At this stage we are in the organisational phase of this entity, a new legal person, i.e. the National Property Resource. Its headquarters are located in the Ministry of Infrastructure and Construction on the fifth floor of the building. About the so-called. location information, we are at the stage of preparation of all technical equipment for the functioning of this entity, i.e. the necessary tooling in terms of staff equipment. That's another line. Today, recruitments have started in terms of specific employment positions, i.e. specialists who will be these real estates in a proper way from an analytical point of view. We have the necessary legal tools for the functioning of this entity. In the context of questions concerning the use of land and the possibilities offered by the Act, I would like to say that as of today, according to the provisions of the Act within 60 days of the entry into force of the Law, local governments with State Treasury land are obliged to provide information on these land. Within 60 days, when we are talking about land within the administrative boundaries of cities, areas that are currently intended for residential construction, and as far as rural areas are concerned, this time is 180 days. After that date there are 30 days to verify this data through the voivodships. In order to allow for the uniform transmission of this information, including in digital versions, in particular as the considerable resources from previous perspectives have been involved in the digitisation of real estate services, we have prepared a regulation which entered into force on 15 September this year and which is the standard of transmission of this information. It has been consulted with local authorities, so here this issue, with regard to the consistency of documents, is clear and clear. Moreover, we held many consultation meetings showing the mechanism. As far as this land is concerned, as I mentioned, due to the statutory fact, from the statutory point of view, the first information, I think, will flow within the deadline after 11 November this year, which is really in 3 weeks. Another point is that these properties will be analysed in a format that will enable the launch of tenders from the point of view of location, the potential of media supply, weapons of land, communication. I think that we will launch tenders at the beginning of next year because of the aspect of the formal and legal treatment of these properties, because we also want these properties to be, as to say, unprotected by any physical or legal defects. Another issue is the matter of land and cooperation with local governments. So the first thing: we asked through the voivodships for local authorities, especially among the municipalities, to disseminate information about the potential of investment property, which are in municipalities, for housing purposes. Today we have more than 700 properties, the local districts' own land, which were indicated by the municipalities in the context of their interest in the location of these apartments. We are at the stage of talks with dozens of municipalities when it comes to specific locations. I think that in the near future we will be able to conclude appropriate, so to speak, factual and physical agreements on this subject with specific local authorities in the area of housing construction. At the same time, we also spoke to entities such as Poczta Polska and PKP SA, in respect of land with which these two entities are owned, which could be used in the formula for payment or free of charge, if necessary for residential purposes. That's the list we have, too. I think we are in the final phase of the agreement on the possibility of using these land, including built-up land, for housing purposes. In the context of local governments, for information purposes, we have created a special website for KZN, which at the moment allows for good, so to speak, distribution of knowledge in this area from the point of view of the local government, from the point of view of the citizen, from the point of view of the entrepreneur. It will be soon, I think tomorrow, the day after tomorrow, a survey when it comes to locations interested, and a whole package of possible questions and answers. We also hold consultations in the regions. We held consultations in Kujawsko-Pomorskie voivodship, in Opolskie voivodship, in Świętokrzyskie voivodship. The next trips will be covered by the Śląskie voivodship and the next voivodship, i.e. in short, we have planned Podkarpackie voivodship, further voivodship, Zachodniopomorskie voivodship. So I think that in all these voivodships we're going to have this kind of meeting with local governments, with entrepreneurs, to interest them. I think that from the point of view of local governments, as regards the talks we are having, the effects of these actions today and after the New Year will be visible. [[♪ The bell ♪]] Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-26-sejm-50-2.u125	Thank you. And the Minister, please respond.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-26-sejm-50-2.u126	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The concerns related to the payment of Polish pensions by the Social Insurance Institution for periods worked in Ukraine are unjustified, and the press information that appeared not so long ago, that Poland, according to the Polish-Ukrainian social security agreement signed in 2012, will finance minimum pensions of PLN 1000 for workers from Ukraine, do not find confirmation. Polish pensions can be granted and paid by the Social Security Office only for periods of insurance achieved in the territory of the Republic of Poland, i.e. for the periods for which contributions to pension insurance were paid to the Polish social security system. It is not possible to pay pensions from the Polish social security system for periods of insurance or employment that have been achieved in Ukraine. For Ukrainian periods of insurance, the Ukrainian insurance institution is obliged to consider the right to retirement and the possible granting of this benefit. This is in line with the principle that each State pays pension benefits for periods of insurance carried out in accordance with the legislation of that State. Therefore, a person who has periods of insurance achieved in two countries may receive a pension from each of these countries, provided that he fulfils the conditions required to acquire a right to benefit in accordance with the laws of the State concerned, e.g. This is a condition for reaching the retirement age or having a minimum insurance internship. As regards minimum pensions, the Polish-Ukrainian agreement does not directly regulate minimum pensions. Nor does the Agreement regulate the increase of pensions paid to the minimum amounts of pensions in force in the country concerned. Therefore, in this respect, the citizens of Ukraine apply, in accordance with the principle of equal treatment, the same rules as those of all other persons entitled to Polish pensions under Polish internal rules, regardless of their nationality. This means that in order to obtain an increase to the amount of the Polish minimum pension, the conditions laid down by law must be fulfilled. Even after they have been fulfilled in the case of pensions granted under the provisions of international agreements, the increase shall be made in such a way that the sum of the Polish pension and the foreign benefit is not less than the amount of the Polish minimum pension. The amount of the increase is therefore dependent on the amount of the Polish pension granted and the amount of the foreign pension, but it will never reach the amount of PLN 1000. In addition, any increase in the Polish or foreign pension results in a reduction in the amount of the increase. To put it simply, it is not possible for Ukrainian periods of employment to pay a Polish minimum pension of PLN 1000. Here I would like to say that this agreement with Ukraine, which was signed in 2012, which entered into force in 2014, can be said to refer to certain standards which are already established and which are applicable in EU law. I am thinking here of the four principles of coordination of social security systems set out in Regulation 883/2004, including the summation of insurance periods achieved in the various Member States of the European Union. As regards the part of the question on the financial consequences that may be covered by the provisions of the international agreement, we have carried out a quick analysis of the costs that our social security system is bearing today in connection with the implementation of this agreement, as regards the question of Members. Total amount of aid to the minimum paid in October this year by the branch of the Zakład Ubezpieczeń Społecznych in Rzeszów, that is the branch which implements the provisions of the bilateral Polish-Ukrainian agreement, amounted to PLN 111 934,63. We do not have data on the amount of aid for individual pensioners, but if we divide the amount I have provided by the number of people receiving this payment, and this number is 263 people, the average amount of such aid is PLN 425. As far as I'm concerned, [[♪ The bell ♪]] of the financial consequences of the whole agreement, of course they were presented to the High House on the occasion of work on the bill aimed at ratifying this document. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-26-sejm-50-2.u218	Thank you. Ladies and gentlemen! In recent years, and during the exercise of power by various governments, various coalitions, we have had and are dealing with a change of borders between local governments in Poland. Such decisions shall be issued following a government consultation by the Ministry of Internal Affairs and Administration. Please state that the practice so far indicated that, when the boundaries were changed, the spatial planning plan, which was in force in the municipality, which most often gave part of its territory to a city or other local government unit, was no longer in force. If such a spatial development plan was part of a larger overall development plan for the entire municipality, then also in the entire municipality, which lost part of its territory, the spatial development plan ceased to apply. Ladies and gentlemen, we have therefore been dealing with the loss of rights acquired by residents who, until this decision, were residents of one commune and, after the decision, became residents of the other commune. This was an abnormal situation, because such a spatial planning plan is adopted with all the rigors of the law, and therefore in due time the plan is presented to citizens for inspection, there is a whole appeal system, the whole democratic system of resolutions of individual committees on the municipal council and at the end of the commune council and is approved by the other institutions. Now as a result of one pull, the plan ceased to apply, which of course created a rather comfortable situation for the buyer of the site, that, by expanding his territory by a fragment of the neighbouring commune, the new buyer, colloquially this name, had quite a comfortable situation, could shape the development in a given area according to his own view. However, we believe that this was a violation of the constitutional rights previously acquired, and at the same time we do not want the new buyer of the land to be incapacitated. Thus, it is an initiative, which boils down to the fact that after the change of borders, a new municipality may introduce its own spatial planning plan in this area, but with such rigors as are required for the adoption of a municipality council which may adopt a spatial planning plan. It seems to us that this will be a situation in which we will respect the acquired rights of residents attached to a larger local government unit, but above all, we will respect the municipality that loses part of its territory, which means we will not bring chaos to the commune, which loses part of its territory, by destroying the entire spatial planning plan. An earlier amendment in this area is precisely a matter of Article. 4, she's already noticed that. Parliament pointed out this when two municipalities were discussed in their entirety. At that time, Parliament stated that the existing acquired rights, i.e. the land-use plan adopted, should continue to apply after the merger of the two municipalities until such time as the plan was amended, in accordance with the procedure for amending the land-use plan. Now that we continue to change these rules earlier, we also extend it to a situation where only a part of the municipality will be attached to the neighbouring local government unit. Law and Justice considers that the acquired rights of residents and the Law and Justice Club should be respected in full support of the Commission's proposal to amend the Municipal Government Act. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-26-sejm-50-2.u219	Thank you, sir. Please take the floor of Mr Jack Protas.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-26-sejm-50-2.u220	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, the Civic Platform presents an opinion on the draft law amending the Municipal Government Act. The amendment provides for the mandatory application of local law in the area of planning and spatial planning in the case of a change of boundaries involving a part of the area of one local government unit to the other. In fact, the need for such regulation results from the dangerous, in my opinion, forceful integration of municipalities into other local government units. Recently, the issue of extending the borders of the city of Opola was loud at the expense, above all, of the municipalities of Łobień Wielki, but also of other smaller local government units, where without respect for the opinion of the inhabitants, without agreement between the local authorities concerned, there was so-called. Enemy takeover. Such conduct is unacceptable, contrary to the rule of law, creates negative social emotions and causes a lack of confidence in public authorities. I would like to remind you that the current law contains regulations concerning the merger of local government units or their extension on the basis of consensus and agreement. So, if it had not been for the sad experience of the last months concerning the expansion of Opola, it would probably not have been necessary to change the bill. In order to prevent the repetition of this type of activity, the Committee on Territorial Government and Regional Policy decided at its last meeting in Opole to start work on amending the law on changes in the boundaries of local government units, so that in the future there would be no forceful practices, no respect for the will of the inhabitants. Returning to the contested bill, the Civic Platform club, pragmatically addressing this subject and knowing that today there are appropriate problems with spatial planning plans both in the municipalities that have given up their territory to another unit, and in those which have taken over these areas in one way or another, will support the draft law. ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-26-sejm-50-2.u226	Dear Marshal, Dear Minister, High Seym! The Municipal Government Act of 8 March 1990 contains regulations on the issues described in the procedated amendment, where two or more units are merged, resulting in the creation of a new unit. It accepts all obligations and becomes a new legal entity, which means that it enters into the rights and obligations arising from agreements and agreements concluded so far and becomes a party to all administrative and judicial proceedings initiated and not completed. The proposal prepared by the Committee on Administration and Home Affairs also proposes similar solutions for changing the boundaries of existing entities, but without creating new entities. Indeed, it would be difficult to accept the differentiation of the situation of the inhabitants according to the nature of the changes in the territorial division of the State, which we are currently dealing with. The legislator assumes that, for an area excluded from the territory of one unit and incorporated into another territory, until new local laws are adopted by the authorities of the unit whose territory has been enlarged, they will be bound by acts relating to the area already adopted but no longer than for a period of 3 years. The regulations contained in the draft law are important not only as regards the issue of local law, which is a multi-stage and long-term procedure, but, above all, from the point of view of the principle of citizens' trust in state authorities. This mostly refers to planning and spatial planning, especially in the situation of a local government unit which has so far absorbed self-contained existence. The municipalities will therefore be able to decide on their own terms on the solutions contained in local legislation on planning and spatial planning in the developed area. The three-year period indicated in the draft law is the time for this local government unit to take appropriate action, to adopt a new spatial planning plan for the included area. In addition, Article. 2 of the draft law assumes that the arrangements set out therein will apply to changes in the territorial division of the State which will take place after the date of entry into force of this Law. Vacatio legis for the entry into force of the Act shall be determined 14 days from the date of its publication. High Izbo! The proposed amendments to this Act include the new wording of the article. 58 § 1 point 8 and the new wording in Article. 70 § 1 introduction to the calculation, as well as giving a new wording to the provision of Article. 70 § Point 1 (7). Article 17 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 The 4th Act states that a soldier is not liable for committing an act that violates only the principles of ethics and the dignity and honor of a soldier. In addition, the provision of the Article. 23 of that law provides for the possibility of withdrawal from punishment if the degree of guilt or the degree of harm to disciplinary misconduct is not significant and the characteristics and personal conditions of the soldier and the conduct of his service so far justify the assumption that, despite his withdrawal from the penalty, he will respect military discipline. It is therefore appropriate to amend the wording of the Article. 57 § 1 point 8 - I mentioned this at the beginning - regulating one of the cases of termination of the juror's mandate, which should take place in the event of punishment with a final disciplinary decision, not in the event of a final disciplinary decision being found guilty of a breach of the honor and dignity of the soldier. Such a change in the wording of the provision will result in the juror's term of office not being extinguished if he is found guilty of disciplinary misconduct while at the same time refusing to be punished. Ladies and gentlemen! [[Adjournment from 10 a.m. 14 min. 37 hours 14 min 48]] I'm reopening the session. We proceed with the examination of item 17 of the agenda: First reading of the government draft law amending certain laws in order to improve the legal environment of innovative activities (print No. 1934). I ask the Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Science and Higher Education of Minister Łukasz Szumowski to present the justification for the bill.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-26-sejm-50-2.u248	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The draft law amending certain laws in order to improve the legal environment of innovative activities is the aim of the exposé, which is to create the conditions for deeper cooperation between science and business. This project is already the second stage of the improvement of the conditions of innovative activity after the so-called 4 November 2016. the first Innovation Act. It was prepared during the work of the Council and the Interministerial Team on Innovation, in particular in close cooperation with the Ministry of Development and the Ministry of Finance. The thematic scope of the draft law was established in the process of extensive consultation, which resulted in the White Paper on Innovation. On-line consultations were held and 340 proposals were submitted and a number of meetings were held with representatives of the scientific community and entrepreneurs and non-governmental organisations. Selected issues due to the specialized and many-required subject matter are regulated under separate laws, and in particular so-called. implementation doctorates, which were established on 21 April 2017, or the Research Network: Łukasiewicz, on which work is underway, and a simple public limited company - works in this area are coordinated by the Ministry of Development. On 26 April, the project was included in the list of legislative work of the Council of Ministers and on 10 October was adopted by the Council of Ministers. The changes included in this proposal are, first, tax changes, an increase in the amount of tax relief due to R &amp; D activities up to 100% and for R &amp; D centers up to 150%. Prior to the entry into force of the First Innovation Act, 30% of personal costs could be deducted from the tax base and, as far as other costs are concerned, only 20% for micro-enterprises, small and medium-sized enterprises and 10% for small and medium-sized enterprises. in the case of other traders. Currently 50% of personal costs can be deducted from the tax base, and for other costs 50% for micro, small and medium-sized enterprises and 30% for other entrepreneurs. The draft law provides for an increase in the reduction to 100% costs incurred regardless of the size of the undertakings. Moreover, in the case of companies with R &amp; D centre status, 150% can be deducted costs incurred. The intention of this proposal is to stimulate entrepreneurs to bear such expenditure, in particular those with financial and organisational capacity and identified research needs. The second important solution concerns the catalogue of eligible costs for R &amp; D relief, extended by non-permanent measures, other than the employment contract. R &amp; D centres receive a number of cost catalogues related, among others. with real estate. It is envisaged that some companies operating outside the special economic zones may benefit from the R &amp; D relief and that the exemption from the so-called R &amp; D exemption should be extended until 2023. taxation of capital companies, limited-liability companies involved in R &amp; D activities. The second area is science and higher education. We propose to increase the scope of activities of special purpose companies created by universities, the Polish Academy of Sciences and scientific institutes on economic activity, to enable the creation by universities and scientific institutes of the Polish Academy of Sciences of Companies to manage research infrastructure, also jointly with research institutes, and to provide the Ministry of Science and Higher Education with legal instruments to monitor the fate of PhD students, similar to the currently functioning instrument for monitoring the fate of graduates, which has worked well and which is an excellent tool for observing what directions they look like in terms of employment. We propose deregulation of the procedure for establishing the Polish Road Map of Research Infrastructure, eliminating the obligation to issue a regulation and introducing a periodic review of the maps. The third area is organisational changes in institutions which create a broadly understood business environment, new instruments to support innovation for the Ministry of Development, the Polish Agency for Enterprise Development and the National Centre for Research and Development, introducing the possibility for the Ministry of Development to announce programmes in the area of innovation of the economy concerning, among others. innovative products, social services, pro-innovation competences or internationalisation - under such programmes, the Minister will be able to provide financial assistance - to introduce the possibility for PARP and NCBR to use financial instruments, to provide financial assistance of a returnable nature, such as: guarantees or capital support. So far, PARP has only been able to provide financial assistance in non-refundable forms, as well as loans, while the NCBR has only limited ability to create companies. Non-tax regulatory changes concern R &amp; D centres. The aim is to extend the possibility of obtaining CBR status at a lower revenue ceiling - from 2.5 million to 5 million, but with a higher required percentage of revenues from sales of R &amp; D results - the current model is revenues - 5 million, including 20% from sales of R &amp; D - clarification of changes in the law on the NCBR, internal organisational changes, clarification of the grounds for appealing members and collegial bodies, or a change in the scope of the committee's activities, the NCBR board. There are also changes concerning patent attorneys, changes in the state of the professional self-government of patent attorneys, exclusion of passive and active electoral right in elections to local authorities in the case of suspended patent attorneys, including as a result of employment in public administration, increased transparency of the action of patent attorneys' self-government, extension of the scope of the Office of Technical Supervision to promote science and technology. The liquidation of the Technological Credit Fund aims at reducing the time of the transfer of funds under the Operational Programme (Smart Development) for the years 2014 to 2020. In conclusion, we would like this law to enter into force 30 days after the date of the announcement, with the exception of the tax rules on R &amp; D centres and the programmes of the Minister of Development. We propose that they should apply from 1 January 2018. I just want to remind you that so-called. The first Innovation Act was adopted unanimously by the Sejm. The project contains tax solutions, which is why we are very committed to maintaining specific standards in the process of adopting them, including maintaining a sufficiently long vacathio legis, so as to provide taxpayers with the opportunity to become familiar with the solutions adopted earlier. We therefore ask you to proceed with the bill as quickly as possible. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-26-sejm-50-2.u249	Thank you very much, Minister. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. And please speak on behalf of Mr Wojciech Murdzk's Law and Justice Club.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-26-sejm-50-2.u250	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! The draft law amending certain laws in order to improve the legal environment of innovative activities, print No. 1934, is the second stage of the actions of Prime Minister Jarosław Gowin, Minister of Science and Higher Education, aiming to create a favourable environment for innovation development in Poland. I would like to remind you that the first stage was the Act of 4 November 2016 amending certain laws laying down the conditions for the conduct of innovative activities. It is worth noting that the Minister for Science and Higher Education conducted an in-depth public consultation before drafting the bill. Their result was the preparation based on opinions of environments creating an innovation ecosystem in Poland, i.e. entrepreneurs, scientists, investors, NGOs or administrations, so-called: The White Paper on Innovation, a full description of the barriers to innovation development. Given the very broad thematic scope of the project - it includes changes in 13 laws - I would like to make briefly the most important proposals contained in it. The first of the thematic blocks are tax changes. As it seems, the most important change proposed in this area is to increase the amount of tax relief for R &amp; D to 100% costs incurred, regardless of the size of the trader. Let us remind you that, before the first Innovation Act came into force, 30% of the personnel costs could be deducted and, in the case of other costs, 20 or 10% could be deducted. , depending on the size of the entrepreneur. It should be considered particularly important to equalise the amount of the relief and, as a result, to waive the lower relief for large businesses. It must be borne in mind that they have more potential to spend on R &amp; D. It is also worth noting the proposal in the draft law to extend the catalogue of eligible costs, in particular to other than employment contracts for research and development activities. In particular, the Minister for the Development of programmes in the area of innovation in the economy, which will finance the introduction of innovative products and services, or social pro-innovation competences, seems particularly important. New forms of operation of the National Centre for Research and Development and the Polish Agency for Enterprise Development in the form of financial instruments are also introduced, particularly the feedback instruments are of interest, which will help to offset market failures by the fact that innovative projects cannot always obtain market funding. Feedback instruments are characterised by providing opportunities to support more projects in a much longer time horizon and potentially renewable sources of capital. It is also worth mentioning changes to the rules governing the procedure for obtaining the status of the R &amp; D centre and granting these centres an increased, 150-percent tax credit, the introduction of statutory guarantees for the transparency of the operation of the professional self-government of patent attorneys and the extension of the scope of the Office of Technical Supervision to promote science and technology. Another area covered by the draft law is science and higher education. The project aims to extend the capacity of scientific units in the form of companies, including the possibility for universities and scientific institutes of the Polish Academy of Sciences to create companies for the management of research infrastructure, extends the scope of the Ministry of Science and Higher Education monitoring of the fate of graduates with PhD students and simplifies the procedure for establishing the Polish road map of research infrastructure. Today's bill must be assessed positively. It should be stressed that the projector not only envisages an extension of the public support system for entrepreneurs, but also proposes a number of deregulation solutions which should facilitate the taking up and pursuit of innovative activities. With this in mind, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice supports the draft law on amending certain laws presented in print no. 1934 in order to improve the legal environment [[♪ The bell ♪]] innovative activities and is in favour of bringing them back to work. Thank you very much. [[Applause]] [[Chairman of the proceedings includes Deputy Marshal Stanisław Tyszka of the Sejm]]

Criminal policy is at first sight, the first association of each Pole, as defined in the Criminal Codes, of repression against those who have exceeded the criminal standard and committed crimes. And so is the design of the defence necessary in the Penal Code. She's got a long story. On the other hand, it is not always in conjunction with such thinking of the necessary defence, which is subject to, one can say, the natural natural human right to defend its rights, especially fundamental rights, not always with such thinking of the particular character of the right to defend the necessary practice, the actions of law enforcement authorities and the judicial practice of the Polish courts. And it is precisely in view of this practice that we committed ourselves even during the election campaign under the good change programme that we will not only make a fundamental change in the way law enforcement acts, in this sense, in order to discourage criminals from committing them even in the pre-field, not only have we committed that, if there is already a crime, the law will guarantee a fair payback for the evil done by the criminal, especially if it is an evil connected with a very serious, serious crime against health, freedom, human life, but also have committed that we will become even stronger and more clearly on the side of those defending themselves who defend their legal assets, which have become the subject of an attack by the perpetrator, the perpetrator, the criminals. We will make this clear by amending the rules governing criminal liability in the area of the necessary defence and crossing its borders in such a way that persons who are householders live in their own home, housing, stay on their own property, fenced territory, have the belief that even if the borders of the necessary defence are exceeded, even if the authorities of the State assess that the means of defence is a means of crossing the necessary limit of defence, those persons will not be liable for criminal liability, unless such crossing would be gross. There is no doubt that there is a problem in the Polish legal practice related to the application, proper application of the necessary institutions of defence, this counter-attribution, or circumstances which, as a rule, exclude criminal liability because the person defending himself - in this sense, carrying out the marks of the prohibited type set out in the Penal Code - is committed an act which, because of the circumstances of this defence, excludes the unlawfulness or exclusion of guilt and is not a crime. It seems that, in many cases, events that should be properly qualified and judged as conduct that are precisely in the necessary defence, as circumstances that exclude criminality at all, whether it is unlawful, whether it is the prosecution's fault or the prosecution's, and unfortunately also that the courts are making a very conservative, restrictive, sometimes and heartless interpretation of the law and consequently those who effectively defend themselves against the act of the assailant bear an undeserved criminal responsibility, and before that they will experience a number of disadvantages related to the action of the law enforcement apparatus itself, the law enforcement machine, later the judicial system. In order to place another accent and signal, also to those authorities, that the Polish State is very clearly on the side of the defending person who is attacked, especially in domestic conditions, the conditions which for each Pole are a space related to the sense of security, and that in such situations it is necessary to examine in a particular way any case of crossing the border of defence necessary, we are presenting this project. Through this change, we also want to make it very clear that a person defending himself in his own home, apartment, fenced territory can count on a greater understanding from law enforcement authorities and has the right to expect this, even if the limits of the necessary defence are exceeded. It is natural that the assailant who commits an attack on such a particular place, which is connected with the sense of security of every Pole, must face the negative consequences of his behaviour. Moreover, by nature, in the vast majority of cases, the attacker has a certain advantage. This advantage is due to a surprise that often accompanies a person who has been assaulted or assaulted. It is also an additional argument that the defender must have additional support, an additional sense of security related to taking effective defence. And yet another very important element that accompanies this - everyone assaulted under such conditions should be convinced that he can effectively take defensive action against the attacker and need not be afraid, need not be afraid, he does not need to be paralyzed by fear and imagination related to what will happen later, when possibly law enforcement authorities recognize that he has committed to exceed the necessary limits of defence. This, you can say, psychological element is extremely important, because emotions are often the dominant circumstances in the assessment of a situation that usually happens very dynamically, connected with this kind of assault, assault, violent intrusion into the territory of the apartment, house, property. Usually it is the emotions that make the way of defending sometimes disproportionate to the nature of the attacker's attacks and actions. However, we believe that in such a situation the legislator is guilty of guaranteeing impunity to the person defending himself. This applies only to situations where, from the point of view of every sober-minded man, this defence is grossly disproportionate to the circumstances of the event. Such situations can also happen. We have, for example, a classic example, known to every student in the textbook descriptions, concerning children who enter the garden to steal the proverbial apple. This does not justify and in no way justifies actions which could lead to significant harm to the health or life of such children. This kind of crossing of the borders of the defence necessary is not justified and will not be justified by such solutions, but in all cases related to the real entry of the assailant into the area guaranteed by the State as a safe area for all citizens of our country, if this intrusion is linked to the willingness to assassinate property, health, not to mention life, always and without exception, the state must provide additional support, additional security to the person defending himself by signaling it: if even by his own means of defence, its intensity, the means which you will use, the tools which you will use, exceed acceptable and today accepted in the light of doctrine, legal knowledge, but also judicial case law, you will not suffer consequences, because the law cannot give way against lawlessness, especially in such an area as should be a guarantee, a sacred guarantee of security for every man and every citizen. It is in the Anglo-Saxon legal doctrine that this is the view that my home is my claim. A view that actually gives the right to all defensive actions that are aimed at the good of the attacker, including the deprivation of his life. We are not going that far, but we are expanding this scope of understanding of the defence necessary in the sense of practical application of this institution. We extend not so much the concept of the necessary defence, interpreted as an exclusion of lawlessness, an exclusion of guilt, also as a consequence of exclusion of crime, as we extend the application in practice of this institution to those events, when the law enforcement authorities or the prosecution or courts conclude that we are dealing with a breach of the limits of the necessary defence, but this excess occurs under the conditions described in the proposed submission, i.e. under conditions connected with the defence of the place which is for each Polish saint and which should be linked to the guarantee of the security of everyone who lives on the premises of their home, apartment or fenced territory. Ladies and gentlemen, we will not escape, of course, by proposing any solutions relating to the scope of the necessary defence, from the use of the marks of evaluation, i.e. the elements constituting the description of what is necessary for the defence, where there are limits to its exceedance, which is related to a certain assessment. Our imagination about the various events that may occur does not allow us to accurately, objectively define the boundaries in respect of each case and any circumstances that may occur, it is simply impossible. Criminal law in this area must by nature be synthetic, not casuistic. And if we choose the path of synthetic shots, we also cannot go into casuistics and imagine various circumstances that could be described in a much more precise language. And this is the way of all modern legal orders in the world, without exceptions, including even the Anglo-Saxon system, which often goes into larger casuistics, colloquially speaking, but also this casuistics has its limit, which ultimately comes down to the assessment of the event, whether it is necessary or not to cross the boundaries of defence. We want to give a clear signal in this submission that the Polish state is on the side of the assaulted, honest Pole who has the right, when criminal action is taken against him, to defend himself effectively at the expense of the attacker's well-being. Even if it exceeds the limits of the necessary defence, it must be sure that the State will stand by it, because the law cannot give way to lawlessness, and the State should respect this principle especially when the matter is carried out in the territory of the place of residence, house or fenced area where the person who is attacked is present and in which area it has the right to expect that it will be protected and safe. It is not always possible for the state to react to the attack by the attacker, by the criminal, for objective reasons, we all know that, and it is not always possible to inform law enforcement and the police that there is a trial or intrusion or burglary. Usually the assailant acts by surprise, things happen very quickly, dynamically. But even if it were possible, the defence cannot be interpreted, as is unfortunately the case in part of the Polish doctrine, in such a way that it is an institution really of a subsidiary nature, i.e. it applies. when and only when there is no other way of averting a criminal's action. No, defence is necessary for human rights, an entity law which entitles each of us, if he wishes, to effectively counter the criminal, to defend effectively at the expense of the attacker's well-being, even if he is deprived of his life in drastic situations. May such situations be as few as possible, and may they be as rare as possible, but we must make it clear that the law cannot evade lawlessness and that an honest person cannot be afraid of a criminal. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

They may be interpreted by one court in a completely different way than another. As a result, the rule may not be effective. It is also important to specify that the rule applies only in the event of an assailant entering the territory of the property. This means that the rule - and this is quite important - does not apply when the attacker is admitted by the victim - e.g. When a trick was used, impersonating someone. Someone got into the apartment impersonating someone else. We all know that very many breaches of the home mire are carried out in this way. The assailant is referred to as an employee of a cooperative or a gas company, an inkasenta. These are also situations worth considering. Failure to take such situations into account is a serious drawback of the proposed amendment. It should be noted that the provision concerns the defence only if the property is invaded, and therefore in practice - it was already mentioned today - a person defending himself against home theft can do so more effectively than e.g. Someone defending themselves from robbery, battery, rape or attempted murder on the street. In particular, given the recent case from one of the shopping centers, it would also be worth taking into account such situations, without denying, Minister, the rightness of the idea. I would like to stress this: the direction is good, but I am showing whether we are showing as a club those areas that could be refined in the course of legislative work. A frequently raised charge against the project is its internal contradiction. According to the novel, the person defending himself against crossing the necessary borders of defence shall not be punished. This does not mean, however, that she does not break the law, but only that she will not be punished for it. And if he's breaking the law, e.g. By using a knife to defend himself against a burglar, the burglar himself has the right to defend himself against a defending person. High Izbo! Unfortunately, these often differ from the general belief among Poles, I also believe, from the view which dominates your statements, that the law should first of all stand on the side of the defender, that the defender should have a significant advantage over the attacker, that the state must not give way to lawlessness, which I would also like to express in such a way that it should not give a specific privilege or advantage to the perpetrator, the attacker, to whom the attacker is effectively defending himself. Unfortunately, the reality, the practice of law enforcement and justice, but also to a large extent with the teaching of criminal law, brings many unpleasant surprises here. That is why these changes have taken place along the way, including the change which you, representing the Civic Platform, have been polite to mention, which concluded that a person who crosses the boundaries of the defence necessary under the influence of justified fear or the uproar of the circumstances of the assassination is not punishable. This change, which was introduced during the reign of the PO-PSL coalition, was prepared and presented in the Sejm when I was first Minister of Justice. It was a change just under my watch at the Ministry of Justice. It was the subject of first reading in the Sejm, which passed positively, but then due to the course of political-historical matters - I will not remind them here - and the premature termination of the term of the Sejm did not happen to the finale. This has been done, which I welcome, because despite the great criticism that I have often directed against the Civic Platform, I welcome the fact that this particular change that we have prepared has been implemented, and with our support. During the PO-PSL regime it was also an expression of the belief that something needs to be changed here, because this practice is failing. Why the changes in practice? Dear Member States, how prosecutors think, how judges think, how policemen think, although maybe to a lesser extent, but also, because they also apply the law, is largely due to a dominant view in law school, which in turn reflects its stigma on the direction of court thinking and judicial case law. And what is this direction? This direction determines to a large extent the so-called. Cracow school of criminal law, including the main creator of criminal codes, among others. The criminal code still in force today, Professor Andrzej Zoll. I encourage you to reach out to his textbooks and comments. Well, on the side of the same institution, the same constructed and understood necessary defence, it brings out completely different conclusions and determines - he and the scientific community associated with it - that defence is not necessary as a self-defense institution, which under all conditions gives the defender the right to take effective defence action. It is an institution that has its limitations on this autonomy. There is talk of subsidiarity, limited subsidiarity of the necessary defence, and even full subsidiarity when the life of the attacker may be threatened. In such a case, if the defender could commit an attack on the life of the assailant, he is obliged to take all other actions, but not the necessary defence. The necessary defense can only be used if there is no other option. This is, you can say, the Copenhagen coup, but in a bad way, which also has a negative impact on the thinking of law practitioners, because it is written by people who construct the law, have a huge impact on its subsequent interpretation. They impose an understanding of the institutions of defence necessary that, unfortunately, it is not possible to take effective defensive action with possible consequences, because it cannot be predicted what is the death of the assailant, but it is necessary to run ahead or call the police, wait for him to arrive and close himself in peace, otherwise there will be criminal liability. Such interpretations appeared on the basis of an understanding of the necessary institution of defence and its jurisdictional form, which has not changed in any way as regards the set of legal standards which describes the institution, but has changed the interpretation which takes place, because the law is alive and subject to some evolution in the context of certain social changes that are taking place. It is about thinking, understanding the social context, the case law of the European Court of Human Rights, to which, among others. Professor Zoll appealed. I totally disagree with this thinking, it's completely alien to me. I believe that the right to defend ourselves necessary is a natural law assigned to each of us. Every defender must be sure, convinced that if the attacker is committed to an assassination, especially his or her special interests connected with a particular place for each of us, for each of you, without exception, regardless of political views, or home where the family is, where you spend your time, where the Poles spend their time, then we do not have to shut down, run away, call the police, but we have the right to take defensive action, we have the right to defend ourselves effectively in a way that is available to us, and using methods that will be effective, and not only those that will bring the least damage to the good of the attacker. Such thinking, which is expressed in this principle of limited subsidiarity or limited autonomy of the institutions of defence necessary, is that of thinking, which often takes actually the point of view of the bandit, the interest of the perpetrator. And we want to say this change: we do not agree with that thinking, we do not share that reasoning. And we want to tell this not only to prosecutors, judges, police officers, we want to tell all Poles. Because I can ask you such a rhetorical question: Is it true that each of you, if you ask yourself this question, imagine the situation that is happening - I do not wish this to anyone, of course - to break in, to rob a house when you sleep and defend your own family, children, people you love... There's a break-in, the assailants are coming in. Would you be sure that you can only be afraid of the attackers and not later what the authorities of the state will do and think, for example. As in the textbooks of the law Professor Zolla, the prosecutor or the court? It's true, as the Attorney General, because we've changed the bill, I have the opportunity, and I'm taking this opportunity to influence the way the prosecutors understand the defence institution, and I'm directing it to the interests of the defender. This is what I do through supervision, analysis of cases that are related to similar stories, training, meetings with the management of the prosecution - such meetings took place - and I always emphasise the element related to the rights of those who defend themselves. I have encouraged the heads of individual prosecutorial units to pay attention to these elements, just as I do, and I demand that they be very firm and harsh in treating the perpetrators of violent crimes against life, freedom - rapists, pedophiles or members of organized criminal groups. The connection to the VAT mob didn't come out of nowhere either. It is precisely that as a representative of the government today I can pursue such a policy, but that does not mean that in every case I want and intend to step in. I only do this when we're dealing with information about drastic violations. This is not possible, because we have thousands of prosecutors, each prosecutor leads several dozen, several hundred cases, and I am not the Holy Ghost. How, then, can we do this? This can be done, primarily by creating legal standards. This is the strongest signal that goes to prosecutors, judges and cops as well. And remember, prosecutors change, governments change. Today, there is a government that is on the side of those who defend themselves, who insists that the defender must have special rights, the government that thanked Professor Zoll and the entire group of his colleagues, who until recently formed a codification committee and gave some way of thinking and tone of action to the authorities of the state, and gave up their work. They played a leading role here, the same was true in the national school for judges and prosecutors, and they were the ones who gave the tone in training there. We changed that. The government and the Attorney General may come, for example. He shared Professor Zoll's views. Recently, Professor Zoll's colleague from the cathedral, Prof. Ćwiakalski, who wrote textbooks with him and fully shared the views that Prof. Zoll expressed, is about the limited subsidiarity of defending the necessary, limited autonomy, and even full subsidiarity, when it comes to the possibility of losing the perpetrator's life. Our proposal may mean a concrete change in the well-being of those who defend themselves, the awareness that they may be in trouble, but also in the subsequent action of law enforcement authorities, supervision of them and the requirements that prosecutors are faced with. or, as a result, later, after challenging the decision, the courts. Therefore, by creating a change in the law, we are making some changes that are more lasting, clear. This is particularly important in the context of this poor evolution that is taking place in the world of science, which is trying to impose the primacy of the interests of the de facto perpetrator. This is not only about enforcing strict criminal responsibility, but above all about the need to rehabilitate, that you must not be led by the desire to retaliate, by the fear that you must always think of the unsub's interests so that he can return to society and that he may by chance not be harmed too much when he is committed assault. Well, I firmly do not share such a view and offer a solution that is reasonable to defend. Unfortunately, the law or even our language, the network of concepts that we use - because through language we create the law - are imperfect. We must appeal to the concepts of judgment, to the common sense and to the wisdom of those who apply this law. But with this solution we move the accent, we point out that we want to increase the powers especially for those who defend themselves under specific conditions. Because we, at all, can say, do not manipulate the concept of the necessary defence structure, do not change it, it is the same in Poland today as it was both yesterday and many years ago. The aim is to ensure that it is correctly understood, i.e. it involves the exclusion of unlawfulness, the exclusion of guilt and, as a result, crime of the act, if there is an unlawful attack and the response to this attack in the form of a defence. We are only talking about those situations that are connected with crossing the boundaries of the necessary defence. And what we want to say is that if a person crossed this line of defence necessary, acting under special conditions - usually in her own home, her own environment, where she had reasons to feel safe, where she had special reasons for caring for the safety of her loved ones, sometimes this could happen, and often so, at night, when the attackers come in to rob him - in such cases you have to give a certain privilege, an additional signal to the defender: listen, you can even take radical action, we will understand that. Because common sense dictates to understand that a defender may be under some particular stress, may be influenced by strong emotions, may, by nature, be very upset by the fact that someone had the audacity to step in, invade an area reserved for her, for her family, for her loved ones, for the people she loves, for whom she has the right to defend with all determination, without later fearing that, in the event of effective defense, including the death of the attacker, someone will drag you to court and charge you. This solution, which we are proposing, does not lead, as the Member was kind to put it here, to the inevitable consequence of putting a person beyond the limits of the defence necessary before the court or, earlier, of bringing charges against him. No, it's not. This design says that this person is not subject to all these negative consequences. There's a thoughtful formula, we used it: it's not punished. It is not punishable, that is, that if the prosecutor and the police assess that the circumstances described here take place and that there has been a breach of the permissible limit of defence necessary even in a substantial but not gross manner, under the conditions described in this provision, then that person is not threatened with being charged with the charge, that person is not threatened with having to go to court later with an indictment and that he will go to court. I will remind you - there are things that are little known and poorly remembered, not all of them are publicising the media, but there are also things that have been made in our memory somewhere - the case of the man from Łódź, to whom the bandits entered at night, into his house, and who ripped one of them out, ran after him and stabbed him several times. This bandit later, fleeing through the fence, effectively, died because of the blood flow, and this man spent many months in custody. And that's the situation. We want such cases not to happen to be arrested, but we also want to have a common conviction, strengthened among the citizens of our country, that the person effectively defending himself, even if this is a serious loss to the good of the attacker, even in an extreme situation, if it is linked to the deprivation of life of the perpetrator, the attacker, will not have to bear criminal liability for it. This person will not be charged and will not be brought to trial. And as for the imperfection of legal matter, the legal tools, we're doomed to do so. We are also maintaining the provision mentioned here by the Member, which was prepared at a time when I was the Minister of Justice for the first time, guaranteeing that, in any situation, not only under domestic conditions, this house, which was mentioned here in a nutshell, but also in any other place in Poland, on the street, in any public and non-public place, a person who crosses the limits of the necessary defence, if acting under the influence of stress, an uproar, which is justified by circumstances, is also not subject to criminal liability.
