On behalf of the Civic Platform Club, I have the honour to present the position of the club in relation to the parliamentary draft resolution on the celebration of the 95th anniversary of the Association of Poles in Germany. The Polish Union in Germany was created as a result of the efforts of Poles who were descendants of mass migration started after 1870. It was then that the largest Polish clusters were formed in Rhine and Westphalia and Berlin. In total, it was over 620 thousand. persons. Also in eastern Germany - under the Treaty of Versailles in 1918 transferred to Poland - lived still more than one million Polish indigenous people. More widely written about this issue, among others. Prof. Ruchniewicz in one of his publications. The first years of development of the association, registered in 1922, is an intensive period of activity in which Polish schools were established, the Polish press was published, numerous cultural and social societies were established, among others. The Bank of Slavs. This is the time, as we read in the draft resolution, when the union actively engaged in political, educational and economic life, becoming a bridge between the two nations. It is worth recalling that by 1928 the Polish minority had two representatives in the Prussian Seym. At the Congress of Poles in Germany in 1938 the truths of Poles in Germany, mentioned in this draft resolution, were passed. The outbreak of World War II led to the dissolution of Polish organizations operating in Germany, including the Polish Union in Germany. Many active activists died, gave their lives for their homeland. After the war, the main part of the Polish minority was found in Poland, while the remaining clusters of Poles were gradually dispersed. It is estimated that in East and Western Germany there were about 150,000 after the war, at least in the first years after the war. Poles. These environments have joined, among others. to reactivate the Polish Union in Germany. The subsequent waves of migration in the 1970s and 1980s have led us to speak of a Polish community of 1,2.1 million according to various estimates. The Treaty on Good Neighborhood and Friendly Cooperation signed in June 1991 clarifies in Article. 20.22 rights of German minority in Poland and Polish communities in Germany. In 2010 the Polish-German round table was launched as a platform for dialogue aimed at fully implementing the provisions of the Good Neighbours Treaty. The last meeting took place in February 2015 and was devoted to the situation of Polish and Polish people in Germany and the German minority in Poland. At that time, a positive assessment of the results of the round table so far was reflected in the joint statement. It was then that both government delegations and the social partners agreed to continue working on issues where progress is less advanced. They were on the Polish side, among others. implementation of the Polish language teaching strategy as a native in Germany or preparation for the renovation of the Polish House in Bochum as the target seat of the Centre for Documentation of Culture and History of Poles in Germany. Today, it is regrettable that the government, knowingly or not, resigns from such an instrument as the round table. If he wants to achieve equal respect for rights on both sides, he should sit back to the talks and step up his efforts in favour of Polonia. It is a pity that the current government cannot really boast of any achievements in improving the situation of Polonia in Germany or gaining minority status. Therefore, we hope that the position of the Sejm and this resolution will mobilize the government to carry out its commitments, because only in this way will the Polish Union in Germany be able to continue its activities, while remaining a bridge between agreement and cooperation. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-26-sejm-50-2.u333	Mrs. Marshal! Mr. Marshal! On behalf of the Kukiz Club15, I present my position on the resolution on the celebration of the 95th anniversary of the Association of Poles in Germany. The purpose of this resolution is evident. The Polish Union in Germany has been operating since 1922. Since then, he has been guarding the interests of the Polish national minority, the Polish cultural and historical heritage located within Germany. He was involved in Polish-German political, educational and economic life. The sign Rodo, accepted by the union, shows the idea that the creators of the relationship were guided by: emphasising the relationship with the greatest symbols of Polishness at that time: Krakow and the Vistula. The relationship worked in very difficult conditions. The moment of the uprising is important. Contrary to the popular position, anti-Polish policy of the German state has not increased since the establishment of the Third Reich. Its origins actually reach the Weimar Republic questioning not only the Versailles order adopted after World War I, but also the very existence of the Polish state, which stood in defence of Poles located within German borders. Presenting support for this resolution, one cannot overlook the basis of the ideal activity of the union, i.e. the five truths of the Pole, adopted in special circumstances, in the face of the German threat to Poland, during the Congress of Poles in Berlin in March 1938. More than 5,000 participated in it. persons. The words of the five truths of the Pole prove the extraordinary courage of the members of the Polish Union in Germany. They're very patriotic. Today, it is worth recalling some of the MEPs, who are literally flying to Brussels and complaining about the country they represent. Especially it would be good for them to remember that our Polish Mother - we must not speak ill of Mother. The historical role of Poles in Germany is borne out by the Palace Radziwiłłłowski, which served as a centre of musical, literary and theatrical life of the Polish minority. Thanks to its dynamic activity, the German Polonia had its economic structures, agri-trade companies, cooperatives and professional union, printers, newspapers, schools, excursions, social care institutions, cultural associations, artistic teams and sports clubs, and even the Central Bank of Polish Cooperatives, so-called. The Bank of Slavs. Thus, historical facts emphasize the important role of the Polish minority in Germany, but also its formally regulated status. It is also important to remember the Martyrology of Poles living in Germany and union activists during World War II. After the German aggression, they were included in a special list of enemies of the Reich, so-called. Sonderfahndungsbuch Polen, prepared by Sicherheitsdienst in Berlin. Mass arrests of Polish intelligence and ethnic cleansing were carried out as part of Operation Tannenberg and Intelligenzaktion. The Polish Union itself in Germany has been delegated and its assets have been confiscated. The essence of the modern activity of the union is the desire to provide aid from the German federal and local authorities, which is suitable for helping the German minority in Poland. Let us hope that the most important postulate of the union, i.e. restoring the status of a national minority to Poles, received in 1940, will be supported by the Polish Parliament and the Polish Government not only by adopting this resolution, but also by their actual actions. The issue of the location of the Polish minority and guaranteeing its rights on the basis of reciprocity as well as the return of the assets belonging to the Polish national minority in neighbouring countries, including Germany, was the subject of my interpellation to the Ministry of Foreign Affairs, the Ministry of Culture and National Heritage and the Minister of Internal Affairs and Administration. Minister Jan Dziedziczak, speaking on behalf of the Ministry of Foreign Affairs, stated that the current situation of the Polish minority in Germany was not the result of the 1991 treaty and the practice of public life in Germany, as well as the lack of care of the previous team for its fate. Minister Dziedziczak rightly noted that the existing asymmetricality in the level of guaranteeing the powers of the Polish national minority in Germany and Germany in Poland does not result directly from the provisions concluded between the states of the treaty. The Minister added that the German authorities' reluctance to grant the Polish minority group the status of national minorities, within the meaning of their internal legislation, could indeed create some kind of organisational difficulties for the German authorities and provide a basis for negative decisions on certain specific issues. Undoubtedly this is the situation of children of Polish parents. This was also the subject of my interpellation, exemplified by specific cases of abuse of competence by the German public administration. The short and general history of the Polish Union in Germany, the actual position of Poles in Germany, causes that before the union there is much more to do. Club Kukiz'15 supports the content and motivation of the contested resolution in its entirety.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-26-sejm-50-2.u336	Thank you, Member. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Please, Mrs Anna Schmidt-Rodziewicz, Law and Justice have the floor.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-26-sejm-50-2.u337	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice on the draft law amending the Law on repatriation and the Act on Foreigners from Print No. 1904. The proposed legislation provides for instruments aimed at effectively helping to solve repatriation problems. The assumed effect of these changes is to increase the number of families coming to Poland every year and, so expected, to complete the process of repatriation. Experiences related to the application of the provisions of the recently amended Law on repatriation to people residing in the Polish House in Pułtusek, as well as comments and suggestions from other persons and entities involved in the process of repatriation, indicate the need to clarify certain solutions in order to improve the process of settling repatriates in Poland and eliminate interpretation doubts. While presenting the position of the club, I would also like to point out the key changes contained in the project. First of all, the amendment significantly increases the amount of cash benefits granted to the repatriant for the purchase of the apartment. According to the novel, the repatriate's family will receive additional assistance of up to 25 thousand. zł. In addition, each of its members will be covered separately. The funds will therefore not only be received by the repatriate but also by the closest members of his family: his spouse and children, which means that the amount of aid will increase several times. It is a huge financial support, which aims to really adapt the level of benefits to the situation on the housing market and thus improve the process of adapting the families of the repatriates to the living conditions in Poland. In order to provide additional incentives for municipalities to provide apartment replacements and adaptation assistance, the applicant proposes to link the grant amount to the actual cost of providing housing in the district concerned. This will encourage municipalities to step up their efforts in this direction. In addition, the legislator introduces the concept of the immediate family repatriate. Spouses of repatriates already residing in Poland are proposed to include an institution of recognition as a repatriate, which will allow to provide, especially for older people, support in the field of pension rights. In addition, the concept of candidate for a repatriate is defined in order to unambiguously identify the persons to whom conditions for settling in Poland can be provided. To date, persons who applied for a repatriation visa before the entry into force of the previous amendment were excluded from the Act, i.e. before 1 May 2017. This amendment therefore extends the catalogue of these persons, allowing them to be covered by the aid offered by the Polish State. With regard to the demands made by our fellow countrymen in the East, the legislator also abolished the obligation to prove that the person was deported or sent by the Soviet Union authorities. The removal of the provision was a serious restriction for those who declared and declared their willingness to repatriate, and the reproduction of the required documentation in the case of many of them remains impossible. The last of the changes is addressed to members of the immediate family of the repatriate, who for various reasons cannot take advantage of the possibility of coming to Poland on the basis of a national visa for repatriation. For them, it will be possible to legalize your stay in Poland on the basis of a permanent residence permit in a simplified manner. To conclude, I would like to stress that the introduction of these changes will be another step in fulfilling the moral obligations of the Polish state towards its compatriots and their descendants who, against their will, were outside the borders of their homeland. These changes will contribute to the creation of worthy and good conditions for them to return and settle in Poland. This is the realization of Poland's great commitment to its compatriots. Ladies and gentlemen! In conclusion, I would like to stress that the Law and Justice Club will support the proposed bill. Mr. Marshal, at the same time on the basis of the article. Article 45 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm on behalf of the President of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice I propose an amendment to the bill amending the Law on repatriation and the Act on foreigners, prints No 1904 and 1959. ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-26-sejm-50-2.u348	Thank you very much. Mr Ireneusz Gains, Frees and Solidarni have the floor.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-26-sejm-50-2.u349	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! On behalf of the Polish Free and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position on the report of the Commission for Administration and Internal Affairs and the Commission for Communication with Poles abroad on the draft law amending the Act on Repatriation and the Act on Foreigners. The bill is to be a fulfillment of the promise made by the President of the Council of Ministers during the meeting with the repatriates on the 80th anniversary of the first mass deportation of Poles to Kazakhstan by the Soviet authorities. During this meeting the Prime Minister assured that the government would not rest until every Pole and Pole who wanted to return to Poland returned. We, as Poles, should give priority to the affairs of our compatriots, who, through the difficult history of our nation, lost the opportunity to live in the territory of the Republic of Poland. The aim of this bill is to speed up the return of repatriates to the country and to introduce new forms of aid, including financial aid. Changes are necessary because, as the report of the Supreme Audit Chamber indicates, the Law of 9 November 2000 on repatriation has, in fact, created legal possibilities for returning to Poland to the persons indicated in the article. Article 9 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1, but for 13 years the problem of repatriating people from these countries has not been resolved. According to data from the Ministry of Foreign Affairs, the repatriation to Poland is interested in about 10 thousand. There is no doubt, therefore, that the process and efficiency of public administrations should be accelerated. In the light of the newly proposed solutions in the draft law, taking into account the importance and complexity of the problem of repatriation, a government representative office for repatriation will be established together with the advisory and advisory body, i.e. Repetition Council. The proposals contained in the draft law respond to the problems identified at the time of the public consultation related to the current shape of the law and the practice of its application. These are, first of all, cumbersome and unclear procedures related to the return of the repatriates to the country, concerns about the possibility of ensuring in the future the destination of the repatriates in the repatriates' centres and in municipal dwellings at the disposal of local self-government units, concerns about the future provision of jobs for the repatriates in accordance with their qualifications, as well as concerns about poor knowledge of Polish language or complete ignorance of Polish language by the repatriates, including the organisation and financing of language courses in municipalities or repatriates' centres, concerns about the adaptation process of repatriates in Polish society - hence the introduction of an institution of the repatriant's assistant. To sum up, the bill responds to the problems and concerns that are currently emerging to the public. We know how difficult it is to find ourselves in a new reality, moving from Poland to the city. It is not easy to find a new school for children, a new place of work. This requires time, especially if the case concerns a person who lives thousands of kilometres and does not speak Polish well or knows the realities of the Polish labour market and socio-economic dependency. It prioritises issues related to professional activation and ad hoc financial assistance for repatriates and facilitates procedures. It cannot be that the application submitted has been examined for 8 years and, in extreme cases, even for 10 years. A state acting in such a slow way in such an important matter as repatriation cannot be satisfied with itself. This is why it is important to appreciate and support this legislative initiative. Appreciation should be given to Members of the applicants who have noted the existing need to amend this law. I believe that the bill after its entry into force will be a really felt good change for our compatriots wishing to return to their homeland. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The Polish Free and Solidarni Circle will vote in favour of the bill amending the Law on repatriation and the Foreigners Act. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-26-sejm-50-2.u367	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the position of the Committee on Foreign Affairs vis-à-vis the government draft law on ratification of the Agreement between the Government of the Republic of Poland and the Organisation for Security and Cooperation in Europe on the status of the Organisation for Security and Cooperation in Europe in the Republic of Poland, signed in Warsaw on 28 June 2017, print No 1872. The need to bind the Republic of Poland to an agreement between the Government of the Republic of Poland and the Organisation for Security and Cooperation in Europe stems both from the need to regulate the legal status of the OSCE in the territory of Poland and from the fact that the Republic of Poland functions as a State hosting the OSCE's autonomous institution. This is the Office of Democratic Institutions and Human Rights, ODIHR. Regulation of the OSCE's legal status in the territory of a part of the countries is desirable but not necessary. Such a need arises when the OSCE organisational unit is located in the territory of the State concerned. The Office of Democratic Institutions and Human Rights, the largest OSCE autonomous institution, has its seat in Warsaw. Mr. Marshal! The Sejm Analysis Office gave the committee a positive opinion on the draft law and on the procedure chosen by the government for ratification of the agreement. The Committee on Foreign Affairs, following its first reading and examination of the draft law on 12 October 2017, unanimously adopted a report requesting the adoption by the Sejm of the Republic of Poland of a draft law on ratification of the printing agreement No. 1872 without amendments. I'm asking you to accept it. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-26-sejm-50-2.u368	Thank you, sir. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear three minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Mrs Barbara Bartus, Law and Justice have the floor.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-26-sejm-50-2.u369	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! ODIHR is the largest autonomous institution of the OSCE and has its seat in Warsaw. The Office of Democratic Institutions and Human Rights, based in Warsaw, was created by the provisions of the Paris Charter for New Europe signed on 21 November 1990, initially as the Office of Free Elections. However, there is now a need to conclude an agreement. The signing of the Agreement between the Government of the Republic of Poland and the Organisation for Security and Cooperation in Europe on the status of the Organisation for Security and Cooperation in Europe in the Republic of Poland on 28 June 2017 constitutes a significant step towards the full fulfilment of the commitments of the Republic of Poland as the host State of the OSCE and ODIHR. The ratification of this agreement is a logical legal consequence of the previous decisions taken by the Government of the Republic of Poland. The political benefits of the agreement are also to eliminate the risk of transferring ODIHR headquarters to another country. I have often heard of such requests from other countries as a Polish representative, a member of the Polish delegation to the OSCE Parliamentary Assembly. It was such a scare that we would take ODIHR from Poland somewhere else, that it could function better there. I am therefore very pleased that today I am pleased to be at the point where we are proceeding to ratify the agreement between Poland and the OSCE. Any decision to transfer ODIHR outside the Republic would be a serious loss for Poland in political and image terms. The location of ODIHR headquarters in the Republic of Poland has become a symbol of appreciation for our country. It should be stressed that it is the largest international institution established in the territory of the Republic of Poland. The activities of ODIHR have been recognised by the international community. ODIHR organises a number of important meetings and conferences in Warsaw, including the largest conference on human rights and democracy in Europe, the annual 2-week Review Meeting of the Human Dimension, which gathered around 1400 people in 2016. A clear definition of the legal basis for the functioning of the OSCE and ODIHR in the Republic of Poland can also contribute to encouraging other international organisations to place their institutions in the territory of the Republic of Poland. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of my Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice [[♪ The bell ♪]] I declare my full support for the bill on ratification of the agreement. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

In the English version it is possible to interpret these entries more widely. Relations will be aimed at supporting the modernisation of the Cuban economy and society, bilateral cooperation and international fora with a view to strengthening human rights and democracy, combating discrimination and achieving the SDGs. The Agreement was initialled on 11 March 2016 in Havana in the presence of the High Representative and the Commissioner for International Cooperation and Development and subsequently signed by the European Union and Cuba in Brussels on 12 December 2016. The purpose of the agreement is to consolidate and strengthen the links between the parties. Relations are to be directed towards supporting the modernisation of the Cuban economy and society, bilateral cooperation, in particular in international fora, with a view to strengthening human rights and democracy, as well as combating discrimination and achieving the SDGs. It provides a framework for strengthening political cooperation and a platform for developing relations in international fora between Hawaii and Brussels. The Agreement is the first bilateral agreement between the European Union and the Member States and Cuba, creating a stable framework for relations between the European Union - Cuba, which replaces the existing ad hoc dialogue, provides a framework and a platform for closer cooperation and dialogue within a wide range of policy areas. By adopting the European Union-Cuba Agreement, which reflects the interests of the parties in strengthening relations, and taking into account the changing regional context, inter alia. The role of Cuba in the Colombian peace process, the breakthrough in Havana's relations with the US, Poland believes that it will contribute to strengthening political dialogue and comprehensive cooperation between the European Union and Cuba, as well as will have a positive impact on the development of bilateral relations between Poland and Hawaii. Poland will also closely monitor the implementation of its provisions by the Cuban side. The issues of respect for human rights and civil liberties should be of particular interest to us. The aim of the agreement is also to promote contacts within the framework of dialogue and cooperation between Cuba's society and the countries of the European Union. We proceed with the examination of item 23 of the agenda: the report of the Committee on Infrastructure, the Committee on the Environment, Natural Resources and Forestry and the Committee on Territorial Government and Regional Policy on the Government's draft law amending the Act on collective supply of water and the collective discharge of waste water and certain other laws (prints No. 1905 and 1960). I ask Mrs Anna Paluch to submit a report to the committee.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-26-sejm-50-2.u403	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Joint Committees: Environmental Protection, Natural Resources and Forestry, Infrastructure and Territorial Government and Regional Policy at its meeting today, i.e. On 26 October this year, they considered a government bill amending the Act on collective supply of water and collective discharge of waste water and certain other laws. The project in print 1905 aims primarily at establishing a regulator that controls the performance of collective water supply and waste water disposal tasks effectively. In the current legal state, the approval of tariffs for these services shall be carried out by a resolution of the relevant municipal council. However, since companies providing these services are majorityly or even wholly owned by municipal authorities, there is a clear conflict of interest. In principle, the operator who sets the tariff also approves this tariff, sometimes to the detriment of the citizen. Approval of tariffs by the price regulator established by this Act, i.e. the competent local director of the Regional Water Management Board of the State Water Farm Polskie, will introduce in relations the supplier - the recipient of services a mechanism for objective verification of the basis and indices of price formation. Also the procedure for the regulator's opinion on the rules of procedure for the supply of water and waste water, a document defining the rights and obligations of the parties, i.e. recipients and service providers, it is extremely important to ensure that Poles have access to these services at the appropriate level. Issues governed by the provisions of this Act have often been the subject of parliamentary debates, particularly in the Committee on the Environment, Natural Resources and Forestry, as well as the audit and relevant reports and conclusions of the Supreme Audit Chamber, which is calling for a regulator in this area. Such discussions were also accompanied by work on the Water Law adopted on 20 July 2017. The law also regulates the rules, I am talking about the draft law in print 1905, the settlement of disputes between the parties to collective water supply contracts and the collection of waste water. At its meeting today, the Joint Parliamentary Committees worked over the draft law contained in print 1905. Two opposition clubs' requests for a public hearing were previously rejected, with a strong majority: 33 to 18, and 33 to 22. As I say, at today's meeting, the Joint Parliamentary Committees worked over the draft law contained in print 1905, introducing a number of substantive amendments to it, as well as a number of legislative and linguistic comments submitted by the Legislative Office. The report of the Joint Committees is contained in print No 1960. I move on to a brief presentation, because of course there is no time for a detailed presentation of the project. As regards the reading of the report in print 1960, I think that each of you has already read it. Art. 1 contains amendments to the Act on collective water supply and collective waste water discharge. Change 1. modifies the substantive scope of the Act, among others. the procedure for the approval of tariffs and the regulatory authority and its tasks. Amendment 4, in turn, concerning Article. The 19th Collective Supply Act contains a detailed definition of the procedure for drawing up, including the opinion of the regulatory authority, the rules for the supply of water and the discharge of waste water. Amendment 5 introduces a three-year tariff period for the services in question, but under certain conditions this period may be reduced. Amendment 8 introduces new articles, in turn, from Article. 24b to Art. 24j, concerning the approval of the tariff, specifying: the time limits for submission and processing of the application, the content of the application, the scope of the information provided by the undertaking, the obligations of the regulator, the method of verifying the application by the regulator, the possibility of requiring the submission of the revised draft tariff or justification, the conditions for setting the provisional tariff, the fees for issuing the decision by the regulator, the way in which the tariff is published, etc. Amendment 9 in turn gives rise to a regulation setting out the model for the tariff application. Amendment 10 introduces provisions concerning the regulator, local property, scope of competence, mode of action. The new chapter 5b contains, in turn, provisions on the settlement of disputes between water and sewage undertakings and recipients of services. In disputes concerning the refusal to conclude a contract or to cut off a supply, or the closure of a sewage connection, the regulatory authority may decide by decision. Art. 2 of the draft amends the Civil Procedure Code Act. Art. 3, I would remind you that it is the result of amendment 2b, introduces amendments to the Law on Government Administrations, which results in the assignment of collective water supply and waste water disposal cases to the Government Administration Department: Water Management. Therefore, all the adjectives of the opposition formulated at first reading and during the debate in the committee, that the law was first led by the Minister of Infrastructure and now led by the Minister responsible for water management, are devoid of grounds, meaningless. I remind you, gentlemen, that the provisions of the new Water Law are entering into force. The provisions of the Collective Supply Act should be consistent with the Water Law passed on 20 July. Art. 4 in the committee's report refers to the law on judicial costs in civil matters. Art. 5 amends the Law of 20 July 2017. Water law by modifying the provisions of Article. 240 and Article 240 of Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 16 December 2013 laying down the rules and general principles concerning mechanisms for control by Member States of the European Parliament and of the Council and repealing Council Regulation (EC, Euratom) No 1605/2002 (OJ L 298, 26.10.2012, p. 1). 490, i.e. the transitional law, in order to ensure consistency of water law provisions with the provisions of the project under consideration. Art. 6 and subsequent articles contain transitional provisions. Among other things, new rules will apply to proceedings initiated before the date of entry into force of the Act. This provision clearly works for the benefit of citizens whose rights in the new wording of the Act on collective water supply and collective waste water discharge will be better protected. This is the main point for which these regulations have been shaped in this way. The new rules for the supply of water and waste water will have to be drawn up and presented within 6 months of the entry into force of the Act. The current tariffs shall remain in force for 180 days from the date of entry into force of this Law. In turn, the revision of the fee rate or the valorisation of the decision fee by the regulator referred to in Article. 24d paragraph. The 3rd Collective Supply Act will be available for the first time in 2019. By 31 December 2017, the tasks of the regulatory authority will be carried out by the President of the National Water Management Board and revenue from the relevant fees will constitute revenue of the state budget. Yes, ladies and gentlemen, in short, the provisions contained in the final part of the bill can be summarised. The project as a whole was adopted by the joint committees with 34 votes against and no abstentions. The joint committees shall also submit a minority request to the applicant at the request of the applicant, namely that in Article. 1, i.e. amending the collective supply law, as amended by Article 4. Paragraph 19 shall be deleted. 1 and 2. High Izbo! On behalf of the Joint Commission: Protection of the Environment, Natural Resources and Forestry, Infrastructure and the Territorial Government and Regional Policy, please adopt the draft contained in the report of the Joint Committee on Printing No 1960. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-26-sejm-50-2.u405	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present the position of the club on the Act amending the Act on collective supply of water and collective discharge of waste water and certain other laws, prints No. 1905 and 1960. The draft law amending the Act on collective water supply and collection of waste water and certain other laws aims to eliminate the problems associated with the functioning of the market for collective water supply and waste water disposal services. The current arrangements provided for in the Act do not sufficiently protect the recipients of services from systematic price increases. In this legal state, the local government also serves as the owner of a water and sewage company, a market regulator, as well as a representative of the recipients of services. Such a solution generates a conflict of interest to the detriment of end-users. High Izbo! In order to counter the above. problems and safeguards of the interest of service recipients are envisaged to set up a supervisory authority for the implementation of collective water supply and waste water disposal tasks. The proposed law provides that certain powers in the area of collective water supply and waste water disposal, which are currently owned by the Commune Council, will be delegated to regional directors and additional powers, in particular: the opinion of the draft rules for the supply of water and the discharge of waste water prepared by the Commune Council, the approval of tariffs, the settlement of disputes between supply undertakings and recipients, the imposition of fines under the rules laid down in the Act, the collection and processing of information concerning water supply and reception undertakings, and in particular the calculation and publication of average prices for water supply and reception of waste water, as well as the drawing up and publication of reports on the conditions for the operation of collective water supply and collection of waste water. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Parliamentary Club Law and Justice supports the introduction of a law amending the Act on collective water supply and collective waste water discharge, but we are proposing four amendments. On behalf of the club, I submit these amendments to your marshal. Point 8 of Article 1 24c paragraph. 1 point (1) (a) b. This reference concerns the principle arising from the Law on the Publication of Normative Acts and Certain Legislative Acts. ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-26-sejm-50-2.u443	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice towards the government draft law on tourist events and related tourist services, print No. 1956. High Izbo! Tourism plays a very important role in the economy of all countries of the European Union, in Poland it is almost 6% GDP is 17 million tourists visiting our country. The tourism market is changing rapidly, new distribution channels are emerging. Therefore, in order to ensure the certainty of tourism services in the event of their failure, we are introducing Directive 2015/2302 of the European Parliament and of the Council on tourism events and related tourism services. The above draft law pursues two main objectives. The first objective is to transpose Directive 2015/2302 into legal order, and the second objective is to improve existing legal solutions, in particular as regards protecting travellers from the consequences of the insolvency of tourism organisers. The organizer or tourist agent prior to conclusion of the contract shall be obliged to provide the traveller with the following information: location, route, duration, type, class, category of means of transport, location, category, type of accommodation, number and type of meals, detailed programme of sightseeing, excursions, price of the tourist event including tax, information on compulsory insurances. Any changes to the contract must be presented in a clear and comprehensible manner to the consumer. It also introduces exemptions from the law according to two essential criteria. Firstly, it is a criterion of duration and accommodation. Events less than 24 hours are excluded unless they include accommodation. Secondly, it's about the nature of the journey. The Act does not apply to occasional, non-profit-making trips and offers only to a limited group of travellers. Of course, business travel is also excluded. The Law and Justice Club has tabled three amendments to this law. The first shall apply to the paragraph. 17. It is about identifying a bank account or an account in a cooperative savings bank. We are extending this provision so that payments can be made not only to the bank account but also to the savings bank. The second amendment concerns § 47, which was very heatedly discussed, so I will quote it: a consumer who has entered into an agreement to participate in a tourist event outside the premises of an undertaking within the meaning of Article. Point 2 of the Consumer Rights Act of 30 May 2014 may, within 14 days of its conclusion, withdraw from it without giving reasons and bearing the costs, unless oral negotiations on the basis of which the contract was concluded have been conducted on the basis of an earlier contract submitted by the consumer. We care about business interests here, but we also care about travelers' interests. ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-26-sejm-50-2.u450	Thank you, Member. Mr Mieczysław Kazimierz Baszko presented his speech on behalf of the Polish People's Party Club in writing. Now I ask you to speak on behalf of Mrs Małgorzata Zwierzan's Free and Solidarni Circle.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-26-sejm-50-2.u451	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Free and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position on the report of the committee on the government draft law on tourism events and related tourist services. I would like to remind you that the purpose of the bill submitted is to implement Directive 2015/2302 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 25 November 2015 into Polish law. The aim of the Directive is to improve the current legislation on the protection of travellers against the consequences of the insolvency of tourism organisers and businesses facilitating the acquisition of tourism services, inter alia. impose an information obligation on travellers. Member States are required to harmonise legislation, inter alia. ensure a uniform level of protection and protection. I must once again stress here how important tourism plays a role in the economy of the European Union's countries today. The dynamic changes in the tourism market force the introduction of current rules that will correspond to today's reality in this area. New developments are not covered by current legislation, which translates into uncertainty in the tourism market. The draft law includes, among others, the following: rules on how to provide travel information and the extent to which information is provided, the rules on liability for non-compliance or improper performance of the contract. In addition, in order to influence legitimate entities, the law introduces criminal sanctions in monetary form. I believe that the introduction of a new ICT system, the Central Register of Tourism Organizers and Entrepreneurs to facilitate the acquisition of Related Tourism Services, will contribute to a faster flow of information. Within the framework of the implementation of the provisions of the new Directive, business trips purchased under the general agreement and travel events and related tourist services will be excluded from the scope of the new directive for less than 24 hours unless they provide for accommodation. I consider it appropriate to separate the rules currently contained in one act concerning tourism organisers, tourist intermediaries and tourist agents from those concerning the provision of hotel services, tour pilots and tour guides. This solution is most appropriate because it concerns two very distant activities. A sub-committee to which this draft law is addressed has been set up for the purpose of considering this draft law. During the work of the subcommittee, the final version of the draft was debated for a very long time and a number of amendments to the bill were adopted. I believe that the new regulations will have a positive impact on the confidence of consumers of tourism services in the providers of these services. The increase in competitiveness should translate into an increase in business revenues. About Free and Solidarni will vote in favour of the bill. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-26-sejm-50-2.u459	On behalf of the Polish People's Party Parliamentary Club, I have the honour to present my position on the government proposal for the Act on Tourism Events and Related Tourism Services, print No. 1784. Following the Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council of 25 November 2015 on tourism events and related tourism services, EU Member States were required to update and extend existing legislation on tourism, i.e. By 1 January 2018, Member States are to adopt and publish the relevant provisions which should enter into force on 1 July 2018. The directive makes it impossible to shape the provisions of the draft law in any way, as its aim was to create coherent and uniform legal solutions for all operators operating in the tourism services market in the Member States of the European Union. The implementation of the Directive requires amendments to other laws. The main changes concern the Tourist Services Act. Part of the provisions of this Act will be repealed, in turn the regulations concerning the Tourist Guarantee Fund will be transferred to the new Act - the Act on Tourism Events and Related Tourism Services. The new law will also be moved to legislation on tourism organisers, tourist agents and tourist intermediaries as modified accordingly. The draft law sets out the conditions for offering, selling and implementing tourist events and related tourist services in Poland and abroad, if agreements with travellers are concluded by entrepreneurs established in our country, as well as the rules for the functioning of the Tourist Guarantee Fund. The provisions of the Act will not apply, inter alia, excursions offered, ordered and carried out occasionally, on non-profit terms, offered exclusively to a limited group of travellers, as well as business trips organised under a general agreement. The essential regulations contained in the draft law are largely due to the definition of the terms contained therein. A broad definition of the tourist operator has been proposed. The concept of tourist service has been redefined. It will contain four elements: 1) carriage of passengers (air, rail, coach, ferry, ship); 2) accommodation (e.g. at the hotel) for purposes other than residence purposes which are not an integral part of the carriage of passengers; 3. hire of motor vehicles or other motor vehicles; 4. any other service provided to travellers which is not an integral part of the services indicated above. The draft law also defines the concept of "Tourist Party' (a combination of at least two different types of tourist services for the same trip or holiday meeting the conditions set out in the Act) and more and more popular "Tourist Party' (a combination of at least two different types of tourist services purchased for the same journey or holiday covered by separate contracts with providers of these services, meeting the conditions set out in the Act). The draft law regulates the rules of operation of tourism organisers and entrepreneurs facilitating the acquisition of related tourist services and tourist agents. The requirements to be met by these operators in order to legally operate in the tourist services market are indicated. It was decided that the Minister of Sport and Tourism would act as a central contact point to facilitate administrative cooperation and supervision of tourism organisers and entrepreneurs facilitating the acquisition of related tourism services operating in all countries that implemented the Directive. The Minister is also expected to ensure a smooth flow of information between Member States, which will concern business data entered in the Central Register of Tourism Organizers and Entrepreneurs facilitating the acquisition of Related Tourism Services and their financial security. The proposed law was transferred in its entirety, but with some modifications, the current rules on the Tourist Guarantee Fund. TFG serves as an additional financial security (repayment of travelers' contributions and compensation for the costs of bringing them back to the country or covering the costs of continuing the tourist event, so-called. The second pillar). About the so-called. And the pillar of the existing financial security system in the event of the insolvency of the organisers of the tourism, this was left to date, making only the necessary modifications. In addition, it will include entrepreneurs facilitating the acquisition of related tourism services. The draft law also sets out the design and content of the package travel contract, including the scope of the information obligations, the principles and grounds for amending the contract, as well as the question of its implementation and the principles of liability for non-compliance or improper implementation. The draft law also provides for penalties for infringements of the law. They are to be measured, among others. for underpayment of TFG contributions or operating an economic activity involving the provision of tourism services without financial collateral in the event of insolvency. There's been a penalty for these violations. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The new provisions of the draft Act on Tourism Events and Related Tourism Services will protect the consumer from the imbalance between the parties to the Agreement for Participation in the Tourist Event. The Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party is in favour of further work on this law.

In the case of teachers, provision has been made for increasing teachers' salaries from 1 April next year on the deadline for making these increases. Here we have also indicated the date of compensation: 1 April 2018. In view of the planned increase in teachers' salaries, it is proposed to amend the way in which the expenditure on the prize fund is determined in the voivod's budgets and in the budget of the Minister responsible for Education and Education. The next point concerns changes to the Labour Fund, which aim primarily at rational spending of funds from this Fund in 2018. The situation on the labour market is very good, as has also been mentioned many times here. The year 2017 is the fourth year in which we report a systematic decrease in the number of registered unemployed and registered unemployment rates. The important issue proposed in the Around Budget Act is the financing of post-graduate traineeships and specialist training of doctors and dentists, as well as the specialization of nurses and midwives from the Labour Fund in 2018. These measures, as in the current year, will also cover the costs of handling this task. Ladies and gentlemen! We are introducing a regulation that introduces a pro-efficiencies mechanism in 2018 in the system to support the remuneration of employees of district labour offices by means of the Labour Fund. The proposed provisions contain modifications aimed at, among others, the creation of an incentive mechanism for the implementation of central programmes financed by the reserve of the Minister responsible for Labour, which also means an incentive to activate more unemployed people and to carry out tasks directly related to the current government policy. Another issue concerns funding from excess funds of the Guaranteed Fund for Occupational Benefits and Pre-retirement Benefits and funeral allowances, together with the cost of handling them. In the project we also introduce a number of solutions that allow the financing of specific tasks from the State budget under the resources available to state-owned special-purpose funds. This concerns financing the preparation of national team players to participate in international competition in Olympic sports through the Fund for the Development of Physical Culture and financing part of the public health tasks included in the National Health Programme for 2016 and 2020 from the Fund for the Solution of Gambling Problems. The draft law also provides for the possibility of redemption of the State Treasury's debt on loans granted from the State budget to the Social Insurance Fund for the financing of benefits guaranteed by the State which are due on 31 March 2019. Furthermore, as regards such important points, I would also like to point out that the Around Budget Act contains a regulation on the non-compliance of the article. 37a Act on Liquid Biocomponents and biofuels, which provides that specific actions and promotion and assistance in matters relating to liquid biocomponents and biofuels and the promotion of green transport can be co-financed by the Minister responsible for Energy of 1,5% planned in the previous year for excise duties on motor fuels. Another thing that has been included in the Around Budget Act is the provision which provides a material basis for the allocation of part of the resources available to the Minister of National Defence to finance expenditure in 2018 relating to the modernisation of the equipment of the services under the Ministry of Interior and Administration in the field of transport equipment, arms, special technology, IT, communications and personal protective equipment of officers. A similar provision concerns expenditure related to the prevention of the negative effects of pilots deficit in the helicopter medical rescue service and the modernisation of the Aircraft Transport Team in the Air Rescue Emergency Service in Warsaw. There are also provisions which, as I said in the same way, allow the financing of this task from the resources at the disposal of the Minister for National Defence. Another, actually the penultimate, concerns a solution aimed at covering part of the cost of contributions paid to international organisations from the National Fund for Environmental Protection and Water Management. The coverage of these contributions results from Poland's membership of international organisations and the direct connection with the implementation of environmental and water management tasks by the Minister of the Environment, which results from international agreements and international cooperation in this area. In addition, it is envisaged to regulate the amendment of the entry into force of the Law of 22 June 2016 amending the Law on Hunting Law. The amendment of the date of entry into force of the Compensation Fund Act is necessary for the smooth, effective implementation of the Fund's arrangements and for ensuring the proper functioning of the Fund and the new system of compensation for hunting damages. A provision is also introduced which allows for increased funding and thus allows the creation and operation of voivodship social dialogue councils. Next year, according to the project, the creation and functioning of each of the newly created voivodship councils is expected to be dedicated to 200,000. zł. Ladies and gentlemen! The bill submitted is reflected in a measurable correlation in the amounts included in the draft State budget for 2018. The provisions of the Around Budget Act are closely related to this project. The legal solutions that are contained in this Act complement the budget next year, allow it to be properly implemented, and therefore I ask the High Sejm to accept it. Thank you. ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-26-sejm-50-2.u564	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice in relation to the report of the Committee on Social Policy and the Family on the adoption of the Senate on the Act amending the Act on Social Insurance for Accidents at Work and Occupational Diseases, contained in the Parliamentary Printing No 1957. The amendment of the Law adopted by the Sejm aims to codify the modalities and rules for the financing from the accident fund of activities related to the prevention of accidents at work and occupational diseases. The Act adopted contains a number of necessary elements that will actually put together the process of spending measures to prevent accidents at work and occupational diseases. The Act stabilizes the amount of expenditure provided for in the Social Insurance Fund's financial plan for accident prevention by setting it at 1% due accident insurance contributions. It sets out the procedure, rules and procedure of appeal for competitions for contributors who apply for funding for actions aimed at maintaining their working capacity throughout their working life. Another element of the law adopted is the conditions that the payer must fulfil in order to apply for funding under the competition. The Act also regulates the process of identifying the entity assisting the Social Insurance Institution in the implementation of accident prevention and lays down the principles of its cooperation with the Social Insurance Institution. It is worth noting that the solutions put in place do not have any additional financial implications. Resolutiond by the Sejm on 29 September this year. The law amending the Social Insurance Act for accidents at work and occupational diseases has been positively assessed by the Senate. The four amendments tabled by the Senate are linguistic and editorial. They aim primarily at improving the legibility and quality of the legislative act. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! My club, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice, supports the amendments made by the Senate and will vote against them. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
